Notice Regarding Forward Looking Statements</font><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Some of
the statements in this Report, including, without limitation, matters discussed
under the caption &#8220;Management&#8217;s Discussion and Analysis of Financial Condition
and Results of Operation,&#8221; of SouthCrest Financial Group, Inc. are
&#8220;forward-looking statements&#8221; within the meaning of the federal securities laws.
Forward-looking statements include statements about the competitiveness of the
banking industry, potential regulatory obligations, our entrance and expansion
into other markets, integration of recently acquired banks, pending or proposed
acquisitions, our other business strategies, our expectations with respect to
our allowance for loan losses and impaired loans, anticipated capital
expenditures for our operations center, and other statements that are not
historical facts. When we use words like &#8220;anticipate&#8221;, &#8220;believe&#8221;, &#8220;intend&#8221;,
&#8220;expect&#8221;, &#8220;estimate&#8221;, &#8220;could&#8221;, &#8220;should&#8221;, &#8220;will&#8221;, and similar expressions, you
should consider them as identifying forward-looking statements, although we may
use other phrasing. These forward-looking statements involve risks and
uncertainties and are based on our beliefs and assumptions, and on the
information available to us at the time that these disclosures were prepared.
Factors that may cause actual results to differ materially from those expressed
or implied by such forward-looking statements include, among others, the
following possibilities: (1) competitive pressures among depository and other
financial institutions may increase significantly; (2) changes in the interest
rate environment may reduce margins; (3) general economic conditions may be less
favorable than expected, resulting in, among other things, a deterioration in
credit quality and/or a reduction in demand for credit; (4) legislative or
regulatory changes, including changes in accounting standards, may adversely
affect the businesses in which we are engaged; (5) costs or difficulties related
to the integration of our businesses, may be greater than expected; (6) deposit
attrition, customer loss or revenue loss following acquisitions may be greater
than expected; (7) competitors may have greater financial resources and develop
products that enable such competitors to compete more successfully than us; and
(8) adverse changes may occur in the equity markets.</font></div>

    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Many of

contained in this Report, whether as a result of new information, future events

About SouthCrest Financial Group, Inc.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Description
of Business</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">SouthCrest
Financial Group, Inc. (the &#8220;Company&#8221; or &#8220;SouthCrest&#8221;) is a bank holding company
headquartered in Fayetteville, Georgia.&#160;&#160;SouthCrest was incorporated
under the laws of the State of Georgia on August 15, 1996 as Upson Bankshares,
Inc. and is registered under the Bank Holding Company Act of 1956, as amended,
and under the bank holding company laws of the State of
Georgia.&#160;&#160;SouthCrest conducts its operations through its wholly owned
subsidiaries (collectively the &#8220;Banks&#8221;), Bank of Upson (&#8220;Upson&#8221;), The First
National Bank of Polk County (&#8220;FNB Polk&#8221;), Peachtree Bank (&#8220;Peachtree&#8221;) and Bank
of Chickamauga (&#8220;Chickamauga&#8221;).&#160;&#160;The Company was created on September
30, 2004 when Upson Bankshares, Inc. and First Polk Bankshares, Inc. merged and
adopted the name SouthCrest Financial Group, Inc.</font></div><br>
    <div id="PGBRK" style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt">
      <div id="FTR">
        <div id="GLFTR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="center">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="PN" style="PAGE-BREAK-AFTER: always">
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">1</font></div>
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center">
          <hr style="COLOR: black" noshade size="2">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="HDR">
        <div id="GLHDR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></font></div>
      </div>
    </div><br>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Bank
of Upson / Meriwether Bank and Trust / SouthCrest Bank</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Bank of
Upson was chartered in 1951 under the laws of the State of
Georgia.&#160;&#160;Upson is headquartered in Thomaston, Upson County, Georgia
and operates a total of seven full-service banking locations and seventeen
24-hour ATM sites in Meriwether, Spalding, Fayette and Upson Counties in western
Georgia.&#160;&#160;In Upson County, Georgia, Upson operates its main office and
a full-service branch.&#160;&#160;In Meriwether County, Georgia, Upson operates
three full-service branches under the trade name "Meriwether Bank &amp; Trust:"
the Manchester and Warm Springs branches were purchased in 1999, and the
Luthersville branch was purchased in 2002.&#160;&#160;In Fayette County,
Georgia, Upson has two full-service de-novo branches which it operates under the
trade name &#8220;SouthCrest Bank&#8221; &#8211; the Fayetteville branch was opened in 2004 and
the Tyrone branch was opened in 2007.&#160;&#160;Effective April 30, 2009 the
Fayette County operations will be consolidated at the Tyrone branch
office.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Bank of
Upson is a full service commercial bank focusing on meeting the banking needs of
individuals and small- to medium-sized businesses.&#160;&#160;Upson offers a
broad line of banking and financial products and services customary for full
service banks of similar size and character.&#160;&#160;These services include
consumer loans, real estate loans, and commercial loans as well as maintaining
deposit accounts such as checking accounts, money market accounts, and a variety
of certificates of deposit.&#160;&#160;Bank of Upson attracts most of its
deposits and conducts most of its lending transactions from and within its
primary service area encompassing Upson, Fayette, and Meriwether Counties
Georgia.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">The
First National Bank of Polk County</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">The First
National Bank of Polk County was chartered in 1920 under the laws of the United
States.&#160;&#160;FNB Polk is headquartered in Cedartown, Polk County, Georgia
and operates a total of three full-service banking locations and three ATM sites
in Polk County in northwest Georgia.&#160;&#160;FNB Polk operates out of its
main office in Cedartown, Polk County, Georgia.&#160;&#160;In addition to its
main office, FNB Polk operates a branch office in Cedartown and another in the
Rockmart, also in Polk County.&#160;&#160;FNB Polk also operates three ATM
machines; one at each of the branches.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">FNB Polk
is a full service commercial bank that provides community-banking services to
the individuals and business in Polk County in northwest Georgia.&#160;&#160;FNB
Polk performs banking services customary for full service banks of similar size
and character.&#160;&#160;Such services include making real estate, commercial
and consumer loans, providing other banking services such as traveler's checks,
and maintaining deposit accounts such as checking, money market, consumer
certificates of deposit and IRA accounts.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Peachtree
Bank</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Peachtree
Bank was chartered in 1919 under the laws of the State of
Alabama.&#160;&#160;Peachtree is headquartered in Maplesville, Chilton County,
Alabama and operates a total of two full-service banking locations in central
Alabama.&#160;&#160;On October 31, 2006, Peachtree became a wholly-owned
subsidiary of the Company as a result of the merger of Maplesville Bancorp,
Peachtree&#8217;s holding company, with SouthCrest.&#160;&#160;Peachtree maintains its
main office in Maplesville, Alabama and operates a branch office in Clanton,
Alabama. Peachtree is a full-service community bank providing banking services
in its primary trade area of Chilton County, Alabama. Such services include
making real estate, commercial and consumer loans, as well as providing deposit
accounts such as checking, money market, consumer certificates of deposit and
IRA accounts.</font></div><br>
    <div id="PGBRK" style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt">
      <div id="FTR">
        <div id="GLFTR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="center">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="PN" style="PAGE-BREAK-AFTER: always">
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">2</font></div>
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center">
          <hr style="COLOR: black" noshade size="2">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="HDR">
        <div id="GLHDR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></font></div>
      </div>
    </div><br>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Bank
of Chickamauga</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Bank of
Chickamauga was chartered in 1910 under the laws of the State of
Georgia.&#160;&#160;Chickamauga is headquartered in Chickamauga, Walker County,
Georgia and operates a total of two full-service banking locations in north
Georgia.&#160;&#160;On July 1, 2007, Chickamauga became a wholly-owned
subsidiary of the Company as a result of a share exchange between Chickamauga
and SouthCrest.&#160;&#160;Chickamauga maintains its main office and an
additional branch office in Chickamauga, Georgia.&#160;&#160;Chickamauga is a
full-service community bank providing banking services in its primary trade area
of Walker County, Georgia, which falls within the Chattanooga, Tennessee,
MSA.&#160;&#160;Such services include making real estate, commercial and
consumer loans, as well as providing deposit accounts such as checking, money
market, consumer certificates of deposit and IRA accounts.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Business
Activities of the Company</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Deposit
Services</font>.&#160;&#160;Deposits are a key component of the Banks&#8217; business,
serving as a source of funding for lending as well as for increasing customer
account relationships.&#160;&#160;The Banks offer a variety of deposit services,
including non-interest bearing checking accounts, interest bearing checking
accounts, money market accounts, savings accounts, and time deposits of
maturities ranging from three months to five years.&#160;&#160;The primary
sources of deposits for the Banks are businesses and individuals in their
primary market areas.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Lending
Services</font>.&#160;&#160;The Banks&#8217; lending business consists primarily of
making consumer loans to individuals, commercial loans to small and medium-sized
businesses and professional organizations, and secured real estate loans,
including residential and commercial construction loans, and first and second
mortgage loans for the acquisition and improvement of personal
residences.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Investment
Services</font>.&#160;&#160;The Company provides investment services through its
partnership with a full-service brokerage firm, and offering its customers
brokerage services for stocks, bonds, mutual funds, IRA's, 529 plans, retirement
plans, certificates of deposit, and insurance products.&#160;&#160;The customer
base for this service consists of individual investors, small businesses, and
non-profit organizations.&#160;&#160;This service is offered at the Bank of
Upson through its division operating under the trade name &#8220;SouthCrest
Investments&#8221; which was created in 1999 and has been in full service since
2001.&#160;&#160;In 2005 FNB Polk began offering this service to its customers
under the trade name &#8220;SouthCrest Investment Services.&#8221;</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Trust
Services</font>.&#160;&#160;SouthCrest also operates a full-service personal
trust department through Bank of Upson.&#160;&#160;The trust department provides
estate analysis, consultation, estate and agency accounts, as well as non-profit
agency services.&#160;&#160;All trust-related record-keeping, back-office, and
securities servicing is provided through a third-party.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Asset and
Liability Management</font>.&#160;&#160;The Banks manage their assets and
liabilities in an effort to provide adequate liquidity, and at the same time, to
achieve the maximum net interest rate margin.&#160;&#160;These management
functions are conducted within the framework of written loan and investment
policies.&#160;&#160;The banks attempt to maintain a balanced position between
rate sensitive assets and rate sensitive liabilities.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Market Area and
Competition</font>.&#160;&#160;The Company operates in a highly competitive
environment.&#160;&#160;The Banks compete for deposits and loans with commercial
banks, savings and loan associations, credit unions, finance companies, mutual
funds, insurance companies and other financial entities operating locally and
elsewhere.&#160;&#160;In addition, because the Gramm-Leach-Bliley Act now
permits banks, securities firms, and insurance companies to affiliate, a number
of larger financial institutions and other corporations offering a wider variety
of financial services than the Banks currently offer could enter our area and
aggressively compete in the markets that the Banks serve.&#160;&#160;Many of
these competitors have substantially greater resources and lending limits than
the Banks and may offer certain services that they do not or cannot
provide.</font></div><br>
    <div id="PGBRK" style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt">
      <div id="FTR">
        <div id="GLFTR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="center">

currently conduct business principally through the Banks&#8217; fourteen branches in
their market areas of Fayette, Meriwether, Polk, Upson and Walker Counties,
Georgia and Chilton County, Alabama.&#160;&#160;Based upon data available on the
FDIC website as of June 30, 2008, SouthCrest&#8217;s total deposits ranked 1<font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 70%; VERTICAL-ALIGN: super">st</font> among

the total deposits in our market area.&#160;&#160;The table below shows our

                <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-FAMILY: symbol, serif">&#183;</font></font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">into
      what product or service market we may enter;</font></div>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
              <td style="WIDTH: 90pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
              <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
              <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
            </tr>
        </table>
      </div>
    </div>
    <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
      <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="center">
        <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" id="hangingindent" width="100%" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">

      </font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-FAMILY: symbol, serif">&#183;</font></font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">how
      we must manage our assets; and</font></div>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
              <td style="WIDTH: 90pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>

    <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
      <table align="center" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" id="hangingindent" width="100%" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
          <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
            <td style="WIDTH: 90pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">&#160;

              <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline;" face="Symbol, serif">&#183;</font></font></div>
            </td>
            <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">under
      what circumstances money may or must flow between the parent bank holding
      company and a subsidiary bank.</font></div>
            </td>

    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Set forth
below is an explanation of the major pieces of legislation affecting our
industry and how that legislation affects our actions.&#160;&#160;The following
summary is qualified by reference to the statutory and regulatory provisions
discussed.&#160;&#160;Changes in applicable laws or regulations may have a
material effect on our business and prospects, and legislative changes and the
policies of various regulatory authorities may significantly affect our
operations.&#160;&#160;We cannot predict the effect that fiscal or monetary
policies, or new federal or state legislation may have on our business and
earnings in the future.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">SouthCrest
Financial Group, Inc.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Since the
Company owns all of the capital stock of Upson, FNB Polk, Peachtree and
Chickamauga, it is a bank holding company under the federal Bank Holding Company
Act of 1956.&#160;&#160;As a result, we are primarily subject to the
supervision, examination and reporting requirements of the Bank Holding Company
Act and the regulations of the Federal Reserve.&#160;&#160;As a bank holding
company located in Georgia, the Georgia Department of Banking and Finance also
regulates and monitors all significant aspects of our operations.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">&#160;</div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Acquisitions of
Banks</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-STYLE: italic">. </font><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">&#160;</font>The Bank Holding Company
Act requires every bank holding company to obtain the Federal Reserve&#8217;s prior
approval before:</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left">&#160;</div>
    <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
      <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="center">
        <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" id="hangingindent" width="100%" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
            <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
              <td style="WIDTH: 36pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;
      </font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: wingdings 2, serif">&#8212;</font></font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">acquiring
      direct or indirect ownership or control of any voting shares of any bank
      if, after the acquisition, the bank holding company will directly or
      indirectly own or control more than 5% of the bank&#8217;s voting
      shares;</font></div>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
              <td style="WIDTH: 36pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
              <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
              <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
            </tr>

      </div>

    <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
      <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="center">
        <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" id="hangingindent" width="100%" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
            <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
              <td style="WIDTH: 36pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;
      </font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: wingdings 2, serif">&#8212;</font></font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">acquiring
      all or substantially all of the assets of any bank; or</font></div>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
              <td style="WIDTH: 36pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
              <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>

            </tr>

    <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
      <table align="center" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" id="hangingindent" width="100%" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">

      </font></div>
            </td>
            <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt;" face="Wingdings 2, serif">&#8212;</font></font></div>
            </td>
            <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">merging
      or consolidating with any other bank holding
  company.</font></div>
            </td>
          </tr>
      </table>
    </div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Additionally,
the Bank Holding Company Act provides that the Federal Reserve may not approve
any of these transactions if it would result in or tend to create a monopoly,
substantially lessen competition or otherwise function as a restraint of trade,
unless the anticompetitive effects of the proposed transaction are clearly
outweighed by the public interest in meeting the convenience and needs of the
community to be served.&#160;&#160;The Federal Reserve is also required to
consider the financial and managerial resources and future prospects of the bank
holding companies and banks concerned and the convenience and needs of the
community to be served.&#160;&#160;The Federal Reserve&#8217;s consideration of
financial resources generally focuses on capital adequacy, which is discussed
below.</font></div><br>
    <div id="PGBRK" style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt">
      <div id="FTR">
        <div id="GLFTR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="center">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="PN" style="PAGE-BREAK-AFTER: always">
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">5</font></div>
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center">
          <hr style="COLOR: black" noshade size="2">
        </div>

      <div id="HDR">
        <div id="GLHDR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></font></div>
      </div>
    </div><br>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Under the
Bank Holding Company Act, if we are adequately capitalized and adequately
managed, we or any other bank holding company located in Georgia may purchase a
bank located outside of Georgia.&#160;&#160;Conversely, an adequately
capitalized and adequately managed bank holding company located outside of
Georgia may purchase a bank located inside of Georgia.&#160;&#160;In each case,
however, restrictions may be placed on the acquisition of a bank that has only
been in existence for a limited amount of time or will result in specified
concentrations of deposits.&#160;&#160;Currently, Georgia law prohibits
acquisitions of banks that have been chartered for less than three
years.&#160;&#160;Because Upson, FNB Polk and Chickamauga have been chartered
more than three years, this limitation under Georgia law would not affect
SouthCrest&#8217;s ability to sell Upson, FNB Polk or
Chickamauga.&#160;&#160;Similarly, Alabama law prohibits the acquisition of
Alabama state banks that have been chartered for less than five
years.&#160;&#160;However, since Peachtree has been chartered for more than five
years, this law does not apply to sales of Peachtree.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Change in Bank
Control</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-STYLE: italic">. </font><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">&#160;</font>Subject
to various exceptions, the Bank Holding Company Act and the Change in Bank
Control Act, together with related regulations, require Federal Reserve approval
prior to any person or company acquiring &#8220;control&#8221; of a bank holding
company.&#160;&#160;Control is conclusively presumed to exist if an individual
or company acquires 25% or more of any class of voting securities of the bank
holding company.&#160;&#160;Control is rebuttably presumed to exist if a person
or company acquires 10% or more, but less than 25%, of any class of voting
securities and either:</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left">&#160;</div>
    <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
      <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="center">
        <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" id="hangingindent" width="100%" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
            <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
              <td style="WIDTH: 36pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;
      </font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: wingdings 2, serif">&#8212;</font></font></div>
              </td>
              <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
                <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">the
      bank holding company has registered securities under Section 12 of the
      Securities Act of 1934; or</font></div>
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
              <td style="WIDTH: 36pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
              <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
              <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: times new roman">&#160;</font></td>
            </tr>
        </table>
      </div>
    </div>
    <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
      <table align="center" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" id="hangingindent" width="100%" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
          <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
            <td style="WIDTH: 36pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">&#160;
      </font></div>
            </td>
            <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt;" face="Wingdings 2, serif">&#8212;</font></font></div>
            </td>
            <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">no
      other person owns a greater percentage of that class of voting securities
      immediately after the transaction.</font></div>
            </td>
          </tr>
      </table>
    </div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Our
common stock is currently registered under Section 12 of the Securities Exchange
Act of 1934.&#160;&#160;The regulations also provide a procedure for challenging
the rebuttable presumption of control.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Permitted
Activities</font>. <font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">&#160;</font>The Bank Holding Company
Act has generally prohibited a bank holding company from engaging in activities
other than banking or managing or controlling banks or other permissible
subsidiaries and from acquiring or retaining direct or indirect control of any
company engaged in any activities other than those determined by the Federal
Reserve to be closely related to banking or managing or controlling banks as to
be a proper incident thereto.<font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">&#160;</font>Provisions of the
Gramm-Leach-Bliley Act have expanded the permissible activities of a bank
holding company that qualifies as a financial holding company.&#160;&#160;Under
the regulations implementing the Gramm-Leach-Bliley Act, a financial holding
company may engage in additional activities that are financial in nature or
incidental or complementary to financial activity.&#160;&#160;Those activities
include, among other activities, certain insurance and securities
activities.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">To
qualify to become a financial holding company, the Banks and any other
depository institution subsidiary of the Company must be well capitalized and
well managed and must have a Community Reinvestment Act rating of at least
&#8220;satisfactory.&#8221;&#160;&#160;Additionally, the Company must file an election with
the Federal Reserve to become a financial holding company and must provide the
Federal Reserve with 30 days&#8217; written notice prior to engaging in a permitted
financial activity. While the Company meets the qualification standards
applicable to financial holding companies, we have not elected to become a
financial holding company at this time.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Support of
Subsidiary Institutions.</font>&#160;&#160;Under Federal Reserve policy, we are
expected to act as a source of financial strength for the Banks and to commit
resources to support the Banks.&#160;&#160;This support may be required at times
when, without this Federal Reserve policy, we might not be inclined to provide
it.&#160;&#160;In addition, any capital loans made by us to the Bank will be
repaid only after its deposits and various other obligations are repaid in
full.&#160;&#160;In the unlikely event of our bankruptcy, any commitment by it
to a federal banking regulator to maintain the capital of the Banks will be
assumed by the bankruptcy trustee and entitled to a priority of
payment.</font></div><br>

      <div id="FTR">
        <div id="GLFTR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="center">
        </div>

        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">6</font></div>

          <hr style="COLOR: black" noshade size="2">
        </div>

      <div id="HDR">
        <div id="GLHDR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></font></div>
      </div>
    </div><br>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">The
Banks</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">General.</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-STYLE: italic">&#160;</font> The Banks are subject
to extensive state and federal banking regulations that impose restrictions on
and provide for general regulatory oversight of our operations.&#160;&#160;These
laws are generally intended to protect depositors and not
shareholders.&#160;&#160;The following discussion describes the material
elements of the regulatory framework that applies to us.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Since
Upson and Chickamauga are commercial banks chartered under the laws of the State
of Georgia, they are primarily subject to the supervision, examination and
reporting requirements of the FDIC and the Georgia Department of Banking and
Finance.&#160;&#160;The FDIC and Georgia Department of Banking and Finance
regularly examine each of Upson&#8217;s and Chickamauga&#8217;s operations and have the
authority to approve or disapprove mergers, the establishment of branches and
similar corporate actions.&#160;&#160;Both regulatory agencies have the power to
prevent the continuance or development of unsafe or unsound banking practices or
other violations of law.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Since FNB
Polk is chartered as a national bank, it is primarily subject to the
supervision, examination and reporting requirements of the National Bank Act and
the regulations of the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency.&#160;&#160;The
Office of the Comptroller of the Currency regularly examines FNB Polk&#8217;s
operations and has the authority to approve or disapprove mergers, the
establishment of branches and similar corporate actions.&#160;&#160;The Office
of the Comptroller of the Currency also has the power to prevent the continuance
or development of unsafe or unsound banking practices or other violations of
law.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Since
Peachtree is a commercial bank chartered under the laws of the State of Alabama,
it is primarily subject to the supervision, examination and reporting
requirements of the FDIC and the Superintendent of Banking of the Alabama State
Banking Department (the &#8220;Alabama State Banking Department&#8221;).&#160;&#160;The FDIC
and the Superintendent regularly examine Peachtree&#8217;s operations and have the
authority to approve or disapprove mergers, the establishment of branches and
similar corporate actions.&#160;&#160;Both regulatory agencies also have the
power to prevent the continuance or development of unsafe or unsound banking
practices or other violations of law.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Because
the Banks&#8217; deposits are insured by the FDIC to the maximum extent provided by
law, they are also subject to certain FDIC regulations.&#160;&#160;The Banks are
also subject to numerous state and federal statutes and regulations that affect
their business, activities and operations.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Branching</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-STYLE: italic">.&#160;&#160;</font>Under current
Georgia law, each of Upson and Chickamauga may open branch offices throughout
Georgia with the prior approval of the Georgia Department of Banking and
Finance.&#160;&#160;In addition, with prior regulatory approval, each of Upson
and Chickamauga may acquire branches of existing banks located in
Georgia.&#160;&#160;National Banks are required by the National Bank Act to
adhere to branching laws applicable to state banks in the states in which they
are located.&#160;&#160;Therefore, FNB Polk may open branch offices or acquire
branches of existing banks located in Georgia with the approval of the Office of
the Comptroller of the Currency.&#160;&#160;Similarly, under current Alabama
law, Peachtree may open branch offices throughout Alabama, or acquire branches
of existing banks located in Alabama, with the prior approval of the Alabama
State Banking Department.&#160;&#160;The Banks and any other national or
state-chartered bank generally may branch across state lines by merging with
banks in other states if allowed by the applicable states&#8217; laws.&#160;&#160;Both
Georgia and Alabama law, with limited exceptions, currently permit branching
across state lines through interstate mergers.</font></div><br>
    <div id="PGBRK" style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt">
      <div id="FTR">
        <div id="GLFTR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="center">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="PN" style="PAGE-BREAK-AFTER: always">
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">7</font></div>
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center">
          <hr style="COLOR: black" noshade size="2">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="HDR">
        <div id="GLHDR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></font></div>
      </div>
    </div><br>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Under the
Federal Deposit Insurance Act, states may &#8220;opt-in&#8221; and allow out-of-state banks
to branch into their state by establishing a new start-up branch in the
state.&#160;&#160;Currently, Alabama has opted in to this provision, while
Georgia has not.&#160;&#160;Therefore, interstate merger is the only method
through which a bank located outside of Georgia may branch into
Georgia.&#160;&#160;This provides a limited barrier of entry into the Georgia
banking markets, which protects us from an important segment of potential
competition.&#160;&#160;However, because Georgia has elected not to opt-in,
Upson, FNB Polk and Chickamauga&#8217;s ability to establish a new start-up branch in
another state may be limited.&#160;&#160;Many states that have elected to opt-in
have done so on a reciprocal basis, meaning that an out-of-state bank may
establish a new start-up branch only if their home state has also elected to
opt-in.&#160;&#160;Consequently, until Georgia changes its election, the only
way Upson, FNB Polk or Chickamauga will be able to branch into states that have
elected to opt-in on a reciprocal basis will be through interstate
merger.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Because
Alabama has elected to opt-in on a reciprocal basis, any out-of-state bank may
establish a new start-up branch in Alabama so long as that state&#8217;s banking law
would also allow Alabama banks to establish start-up branches in that
state.&#160;&#160;Similarly, Peachtree may establish a new start-up branch in
any state that has elected to opt-in on a reciprocal basis.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Prompt Corrective
Action</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-STYLE: italic">.&#160;&#160;</font>The Federal
Deposit Insurance Corporation Improvement Act of 1991 establishes a system of
prompt corrective action to resolve the problems of undercapitalized financial
institutions.&#160;&#160;Under this system, the federal banking regulators have
established five capital categories (well capitalized, adequately capitalized,
undercapitalized, significantly undercapitalized and critically
undercapitalized) in which all institutions are placed.&#160;&#160;The federal
banking agencies have also specified by regulation the relevant capital levels
for each of the other categories.&#160;&#160;At December 31, 2008, the Banks
qualified for the well-capitalized category<font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">.</font></font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Federal
banking regulators are required to take various mandatory supervisory actions
and are authorized to take other discretionary actions with respect to
institutions in the three undercapitalized categories.&#160;&#160;The severity
of the action depends upon the capital category in which the institution is
placed.&#160;&#160;Generally, subject to a narrow exception, the banking
regulator must appoint a receiver or conservator for an institution that is
critically undercapitalized.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">FDIC Insurance
Assessments.</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-STYLE: italic">&#160;</font>The FDIC is an
independent agency of the United States government that uses the Deposit
Insurance Fund to protect against the loss of insured deposits if an
FDIC-insured bank or savings association fails. The FDIC must maintain the
Deposit Insurance Fund within a range between 1.15 percent and 1.50 percent of
all insured deposits.&#160;The FDIC has adopted a risk-based assessment system
for insured depository institutions that <font style="DISPLAY: inline">takes
into account</font> the risks attributable to different categories and
concentrations of assets and liabilities.&#160;&#160;The system <font style="DISPLAY: inline">assesses</font> higher rates on those institutions that
pose greater risks to the Deposit Insurance Fund<font style="DISPLAY: inline">
(the </font><font style="DISPLAY: inline">&#8220;</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline">DIF</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline">&#8221;</font><font style="DISPLAY: inline">)</font>.&#160;&#160;The FDIC places each institution in
one of four risk categories using a two-step process based first on capital
ratios (the capital group assignment) and then on other relevant information
(the supervisory group assignment).&#160;&#160;Within the lowest risk category,
Risk Category I, rates vary based on each institution&#8217;s CAMELS component
ratings, certain financial ratios, and long-term debt issuer ratings, if
any.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left">&#160;</div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Capital
group assignments are made quarterly and an institution is assigned to one of
three capital categories: (1) well capitalized,; (2) adequately capitalized; and
(3) undercapitalized.&#160;&#160;These three categories are substantially
similar to the prompt corrective action categories described above, with the
&#8220;undercapitalized&#8221; category including institutions that are undercapitalized,
significantly undercapitalized, and critically undercapitalized for prompt
corrective action purposes.&#160;&#160;The FDIC also assigns an institution to
one of three supervisory subgroups based on a supervisory evaluation that the
institution&#8217;s primary federal banking regulator provides to the FDIC and
information that the FDIC determines to be relevant to the institution&#8217;s
financial condition and the risk posed to the deposit insurance
funds.&#160;&#160;For 2008, assessments ranged from 5 to 43 cents per $100 of
deposits, depending on the institution&#8217;s risk category. capital group and
supervisory subgroup. Institutions in the lowest risk category, Risk Category I,
were charged a rate between 5 and 7 cents per $100 of deposits.&#160;&#160;Risk
Categories II, III, and IV were charged 10 basis points, 28 basis points and 43
basis points, respectively.</font></div><br>
    <div id="PGBRK" style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt">
      <div id="FTR">
        <div id="GLFTR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="center">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="PN" style="PAGE-BREAK-AFTER: always">
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">8</font></div>
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center">
          <hr style="COLOR: black" noshade size="2">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="HDR">
        <div id="GLHDR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></font></div>
      </div>
    </div><br>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Because
the Deposit Insurance Fund reserve fell below 1.15 percent as of June 30, 2008,
and was expected to remain below 1.15 percent, the Federal Deposit Insurance
Reform Act of 2005 required the FDIC to establish and implement a restoration
plan to restore the reserve ratio to no less than 1.15 percent within five
years, absent extraordinary circumstances.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">On
December 16, 2008, the FDIC adopted, as part of its restoration plan, a uniform
increase to the assessment rates by 7 basis points (annualized) for the first
quarter 2009 assessments.&#160;&#160;As a result, institutions in Risk Category
I will be charged a rate between 12 and 14 cents per $100 of
deposits.&#160;&#160;Risk Categories II, III, and IV will be charged 17 basis
points, 35 basis points, and 50 basis points, respectively.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">On
February 27, 2009, the FDIC amended its restoration plan to extend the period
for restoration to seven years and further revised the risk-based assessment
system.&#160;&#160;Starting with the second quarter of 2009, institutions in
Risk Category I will have a base assessment rate between 12 and 16 cents per
$100 of deposits.&#160;&#160;Risk Categories II, III, and IV will be have base
assessment rates of 22 basis points, 32 basis points, and 45 basis points,
respectively.&#160;&#160;These base assessments will be subject to adjustments
based on each institution&#8217;s unsecured debt, secured liabilities, and use of
brokered deposits.&#160;&#160;As a result of these adjustments, institutions in
Risk Category I will be charged rate between 7 and 24 cents per $100 of
deposits.&#160;&#160;Risk Categories II, III, and IV will be charged between 17
and 43 basis points, 27 and 58 basis points, and 40 and 77.5 basis points,
respectively.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="justify"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">Under an
interim rule adopted on February 27, 2009, the FDIC will impose an emergency
special assessment of 20 basis points as of June 30, 2009, and may impose
additional emergency special assessments of up to 10 basis points thereafter if
the reserve ratio is estimated to fall to a level that the FDIC believes would
adequately affect public confidence or to a level that shall be close to zero or
negative at the end of a calendar quarter.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">The FDIC
may, without further notice-and-comment rulemaking, adopt rates that are higher
or lower than the stated base assessment rates, provided that the FDIC cannot
(i) increase or decrease the total rates from one quarter to the next by more
than three basis points, or (ii) deviate by more than three basis points from
the stated assessment rates.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">The FDIC
may terminate its insurance of deposits if it finds that the institution has
engaged in unsafe and unsound practices, is in an unsafe or unsound condition to
continue operations, or has violated any applicable law, regulation, rule,
order<font style="DISPLAY: inline; TEXT-DECORATION: line-through">,</font> or
condition imposed by the FDIC.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">FDIC Temporary
Liquidity Guarantee Program.&#160;&#160;</font>On October 14, 2008, the FDIC
announced that its Board of Directors, under the authority to prevent &#8220;systemic
risk&#8221; in the U.S. banking system, approved the Temporary Liquidity Guarantee
Program (&#8220;TLGP&#8221;). The purpose of the TLGP is to strengthen confidence and
encourage liquidity in the banking system.&#160;&#160;The TLGP is composed of
two components, the Debt Guarantee Program and the Transaction Account Guarantee
Program, and institutions had the opportunity, prior to December 5, 2008, to
opt-out of either or both components of the TLGP.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-STYLE: italic">The Debt Guarantee
Program.</font>&#160;Under the TLGP, the FDIC is permitted to guarantee certain
newly issued senior unsecured debt issued by participating financial
institutions.&#160;&#160;The annualized fee that the FDIC will assess to
guarantee the senior unsecured debt varies by the length of maturity of the
debt.&#160;&#160;For debt with a maturity of 180 days or less (excluding
overnight debt), the fee is 50 basis points; for debt with a maturity between
181 days and 364 days, the fee is 75 basis points, and for debt with a maturity
of 365 days or longer, the fee is 100 basis points.&#160;&#160;The Bank did not
opt-out of the Debt Guarantee component of the TLGP.</font></div><br>
    <div id="PGBRK" style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt">
      <div id="FTR">
        <div id="GLFTR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="center">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="PN" style="PAGE-BREAK-AFTER: always">
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">9</font></div>
        <div style="WIDTH: 100%; TEXT-ALIGN: center">
          <hr style="COLOR: black" noshade size="2">
        </div>
      </div>
      <div id="HDR">
        <div id="GLHDR" style="WIDTH: 100%" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></font></div>
      </div>
    </div><br>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="justify"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-STYLE: italic">The Transaction Account Guarantee
Program</font>. Under the TLGP, the FDIC is permitted to fully insure
non-interest bearing deposit accounts held at participating FDIC-insured
institutions, regardless of dollar amount. The temporary guarantee will expire
at the end of 2009.&#160;&#160;For the eligible non-interest-bearing transaction
deposit accounts (including accounts swept from a non-interest bearing
transaction account into an non-interest bearing savings deposit account), a 10
basis point annual rate surcharge will be applied to non-bearing transaction
deposit amounts over $250,000. Institutions will not be assessed on amounts that
are otherwise insured.&#160;&#160;The Bank did not opt-out of the Transaction
Account Guarantee component of the TLGP.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Community
Reinvestment Act.&#160;&#160;</font>The Community Reinvestment Act requires
that, in connection with examinations of financial institutions within their
respective jurisdictions, the federal bank regulators shall evaluate the record
of each financial institution in meeting the credit needs of its local
community, including low and moderate-income neighborhoods.&#160;&#160;These
facts are also considered in evaluating mergers, acquisitions and applications
to open a branch or facility.&#160;&#160;Failure to adequately meet these
criteria could impose additional requirements and limitations on the
Banks.&#160;&#160;Additionally, the Banks must publicly disclose the terms of
various Community Reinvestment Act-related agreements.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Allowance for
Loan and Lease Losses.&#160;&#160;</font>The Allowance for Loan and Lease Losses
(the &#8220;ALLL&#8221;) represents one of the most significant estimates in the Bank&#8217;s
financial statements and regulatory reports.&#160;&#160;Because of its
significance, the Banks have developed systems by which they develop, maintain
and document comprehensive, systematic and consistently applied processes for
determining the amounts of the ALLL and the provision for loan and lease
losses.&#160;&#160;The Interagency Policy Statement on the Allowance for Loan
and Lease Losses, issued on December 13, 2006, encourages all banks to ensure
controls are in place to consistently determine the ALLL in accordance with
GAAP, the bank&#8217;s stated policies and procedures, management&#8217;s best judgment and
relevant supervisory guidance.&#160;&#160;Consistent with supervisory guidance,
the Banks maintain a prudent and conservative, but not excessive, ALLL, that is
at a level appropriate to cover estimated credit losses on individually
evaluated loans determined to be impaired as well as estimated credit losses
inherent in the remainder of the loan and lease portfolio.&#160;&#160;The Banks&#8217;
estimates of credit losses reflect consideration of all significant factors that
affect the collectibility of the portfolio as of the evaluation
date.&#160;&#160;See &#8220;Management&#8217;s Discussion and Analysis &#8211; Critical Accounting
Policies.&#8221;</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-WEIGHT: bold; FONT-STYLE: italic">Commercial Real
Estate Lending</font>.&#160;&#160;The Banks&#8217; lending operations may be subject
to enhanced scrutiny by federal banking regulators based on their concentrations
of commercial real estate loans.&#160;&#160;On December 6, 2006, the federal
banking regulators issued final guidance to remind financial institutions of the
risk posed by commercial real estate (&#8220;CRE&#8221;) lending
concentrations.&#160;&#160;CRE loans generally include land development,
construction loans and loans secured by multifamily property, and nonfarm,
nonresidential real property where the primary source of repayment is derived
from rental income associated with the property.</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">The
guidance prescribes the following guidelines for its examiners to help identify
institutions that are potentially exposed to significant CRE risk and may
warrant greater supervisory scrutiny:</font></div>
    <div style="DISPLAY: block; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><br></div>
    <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
      <table align="center" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" id="hangingindent" width="100%" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
          <tr valign="top" style="TEXT-ALIGN: left;">
            <td style="WIDTH: 36pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman">&#160;
      </font></div>
            </td>
            <td style="WIDTH: 18pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="MARGIN-LEFT: 0pt; TEXT-INDENT: 0pt; MARGIN-RIGHT: 0pt; TEXT-ALIGN: left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline;" face="Wingdings 2, serif">&#8212;</font></font></div>
            </td>
            <td style="TEXT-ALIGN: left">
              <div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left" align="left"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-FAMILY: Times New Roman"><font style="DISPLAY: inline; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">total reported loans for
      construction, land development and other land represent 100% or more of
      the institutions total capital,
or</font></font></div>
            </td>
          </tr>
      </table>
    </div><br>
